\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma_{ad}}{a}[\mathcal{D}^{s-1},\rho u^{0}L_{X}]v^{i}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}}{a}[\mathcal{D}^{s-1},\rho v^{j}\nabla_{j}]v^{i}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}}{a}\mathcal{D}^{s-1}\left[\frac{\rho[1+g(v,v)]}{N}\nabla^{i}N\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{a}\left\{[\mathcal{D}^{s-1},\nabla^{i}]\rho+[\mathcal{D}^{s-1},v^{i}L_{v}]\rho+a[\mathcal{D}^{s-1},u^{0}v^{i}\partial_{t}]\rho-[\mathcal{D}^{s-1},u^{0}v^{i}L_{X}]\rho\right\}.

Notice that we have only retained the principal and decay generating terms in the evolution equations for commuted entities and collected remaining terms as
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
. These terms need to be estimated carefully, which we will do in a later section. Now we define the energy current. The components of the energy current
\mathcal{C}
associated with the commuted fluid variables are defined as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{0}:=\frac{(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}^{2}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\frac{g_{ij}v^{i}\dot{v}^{j}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\dot{\rho}

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left(-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{i}:=\frac{(\gamma_{ad}-1)v^{i}\dot{\rho}^{2}}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\dot{v}^{i}\dot{\rho}+\gamma_{ad}\rho v^{i}(-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}).

(83)

The energy current vector field is
\mathcal{C}:=C^{0}\partial_{t}+\frac{1}{a}\mathcal{C}^{i}\partial_{i}
. Now we show how this energy current may be used to obtain an energy. The energy is naturally defined by contracting the current with the unit time-like Cauchy hypersurface orthogonal vector field
\mathbf{n}:=\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}-\frac{1}{aN}X

\displaystyle E_{fluid}:=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{C}^{\mu}\mathbf{n}_{\mu}\mu_{g},

(84)

positivity, coercivity, and convexity of which will be shown in lemma (2). We need to define an energy associated with the gravitational degrees of freedom as well. Motivated by the energy associated with the linear stability analysis
[
23
]
, we define a natural wave equation type of energy (which can be read off from the evolution equations) for the gravitational degrees of freedom introduced by
[
28
]

\displaystyle E_{Ein}=\sum_{I=1}^{s}\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle k^{tr}|\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{8}\langle u|\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u\rangle_{L^{2}}\right).

(85)

One may explicitly write down the lowest order gravitational energy as follows

\displaystyle E^{1}_{Ein}

(86)

\displaystyle\ =\frac{1}{8}\int_{M}(\nabla[\gamma]_{k}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}u_{mn}g^{kl}\gamma^{im}\gamma^{jn}+(R[\gamma]^{k}~_{ij}~{}^{m}+R[\gamma]^{k}~_{ji}~{}^{m})u_{km}u_{ln}\gamma^{il}\gamma^{jn})\mu_{g}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}.

We want to establish the coercive property of these energies. In other words we would like to show that these energies control the desired norm of the data
(u,k^{tr},\dot{\rho},\dot{v})
. Let us define the total energy as follows

\displaystyle E_{total}:=E_{fluid}+E_{Ein}

(87)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}^{2}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\frac{g_{ij}v^{i}\dot{v}^{j}}{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\left(-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}\right)\right)\mu_{g}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle k^{tr}|\mathcal{L}^{i-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{8}\langle u|\mathcal{L}^{i}_{g,\gamma}u\rangle_{L^{2}}\right).

The following two lemmas establish the coercive property of the energy for small data. Let us write
u=g-\gamma
as follows

\displaystyle u=g-\gamma=u^{||}+u^{\perp}+z,

(88)

where
u^{||}\in\ker(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma})
,
u^{\perp}\perp\ker(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma})
and
||z||_{H^{s}}\lesssim||u^{||}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}
. In particular,
u^{||},u^{\perp}\in C^{TT}(S^{2}\Sigma)
.

Lemma 1:

Let (
\gamma,g,k^{tr}
) be such that
(g,\gamma)
satisfy the shadow gauge and
g-\gamma=u
. There exists
\delta>0
sufficiently small, and a constant
C=C(\delta)>0
such that if
(u,k^{tr})\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\in H^{s}\times H^{s-1}
,
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
the following estimate holds

\displaystyle||u^{||}||_{H^{s}}\leq C\left(||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\right).

(89)

Proof:
Following the shadow gauge (
u
is
L^{2}
-orthogonal to
\mathcal{N}
) (
72
), we may write

\displaystyle\langle(g-\gamma),h^{TT||}+L_{Y^{||}}\gamma\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(90)

\displaystyle\langle u^{||}+u^{\perp}+z,h^{TT||}+L_{Y^{||}}\gamma\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\langle u^{||},h^{TT||}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\langle z,h^{TT||}+L_{Y^{||}}\gamma\rangle_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

where we have used the facts that
\langle u^{\perp},h^{TT||}\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
and
\langle u^{||}+u^{\perp},L_{Y^{||}}\gamma\rangle_{L^{2}}=0
. Using the relation obtained, we may say that
u^{||}
is a smooth function of
z
which satisfies

\displaystyle||z||_{H^{s}}\leq C(||u^{||}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}),

(91)

and therefore,
u^{||}
satisfies the following estimate

\displaystyle||u^{||}||_{H^{s}}\leq C\left(||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\right).~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Box

(92)

Lemma 2:

Let
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
,
\gamma\in\mathcal{N}
, and
E_{total}
be the the total energy defined in (
87
) and
\gamma_{ad}\in(1,\frac{n+1}{n})
. Then
\exists~\delta>0,C=C(\delta)>0
, such that
\forall(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in\mathcal{B}_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\in H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
, the following estimate holds

\displaystyle||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||\delta\rho||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\leq CE_{total}.

(93)

Proof:
Firstly notice that the following holds by definition

\displaystyle||\delta\rho||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}=||\dot{\rho}||^{2}_{L^{2}},~||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}=||\dot{v}||^{2}_{L^{2}}.

(94)

First we show that the total energy is positive definite. Assuming
spec\{\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}\}>0
, it follows that the gravitational energy is non-negative definite i.e.,

\displaystyle E_{Ein}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\frac{1}{2}\langle k^{tr}|\mathcal{L}^{i-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{8}\langle u|\mathcal{L}^{i}_{g,\gamma}u\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)\geq 0

(95)

for
u\notin\ker(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma})
and under the small data assumption. The equality holds only for
u\equiv 0,~k^{tr}\equiv 0
.
The non-negativity of the fluid energy follows due to the following calculations. Cauchy-Schwartz yields
g(v,\dot{v})^{2}\leq g(v,v)g(\dot{v},\dot{v})
and therefore

\displaystyle-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}\geq g(\dot{v},\dot{v})-\frac{g(v,v)g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}=\frac{g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}

(96)

and

\displaystyle-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}\leq g(\dot{v},\dot{v})+\frac{g(v,v)g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}=\frac{g(\dot{v},\dot{v})(1+2g(v,v))}{1+g(v,v)}.

Now consider the following

\displaystyle 2\frac{g_{ij}v^{i}\dot{v}^{j}}{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\dot{\rho}\leq\frac{\dot{\rho}^{2}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\alpha(n)\gamma_{ad}\rho}+\frac{\alpha(n)\gamma_{ad}\rho g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{(1+g(v,v))^{3/2}}

(97)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\dot{\rho}^{2}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\alpha(n)\gamma_{ad}\rho}+\frac{\alpha(n)\gamma_{ad}\rho g(v,v)g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{(1+g(v,v))^{3/2}}

which yields

\displaystyle E_{fluid}\geq

(98)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}(1-\frac{1}{\alpha(n)})[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}^{2}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\alpha(n)\gamma_{ad}(\gamma_{ad}-1)\rho g(v,v)g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{(1+g(v,v))^{3/2}}\right)\mu_{g}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}(1-\frac{1}{\alpha(n)})[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho g(\dot{v},\dot{v})}{(1+g(v,v))^{3/2}}\left(1+g(v,v)-\alpha(n)(\gamma_{ad}-1)g(v,v)\right)\right)\mu_{g}\geq 0

provided
\alpha(n)(\gamma_{ad}-1)\leq 1
and
\alpha(n)>1
. The assumption
\gamma_{ad}<\frac{n+1}{n}
yields a solution
\alpha(n)
depending only on the dimension
n
.

We now compute the Hessian of
E_{total}
at
(u,k^{tr},\dot{\rho},\dot{v})=(0,0,0,0)
for variations
(p,q,r,y)
and obtain

\displaystyle D^{2}E_{total}|_{(0,0,0,0)}\cdot((p,q,r,y),(p,q,r,y))=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\left(\frac{1}{4}\langle p,\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\gamma,\gamma}p\rangle_{L^{2}}+\langle q,\mathcal{L}^{i-1}_{\gamma,\gamma}q\rangle_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}(\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}r^{2}+\gamma_{ad}C_{\rho}\gamma(y,y))\mu_{\gamma}\geq 0

and equality holds if and only if
(q,r,y)=(0,0,0)
and
p\in\ker(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma})
. Therefore,
D^{2}E_{total}|_{(0,0,0,0)}:(H^{s}-\ker(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}))\times H^{s-1}\times L^{2}\times L^{2}\to Image(D^{2}E_{total}|_{(0,0,0,0)})
is an isomorphism yielding

\displaystyle||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||\dot{\rho}||^{2}_{L^{2}}+||\dot{v}||^{2}_{L^{2}}

(99)

\displaystyle\leq CD^{2}E_{total}|_{(0,0,0,0)}\cdot((p,q,r,y),(p,q,r,y))

which upon utilizing the Hilbert space version of the Morse lemma (or Taylor’s theorem) yields

\displaystyle||u^{\perp}||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||\delta\rho||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\leq CE_{total}.~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Box

(100)

Now using the previous lemma (lemma 1), we obtain the following coercive property of the total energy

\displaystyle||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||\delta\rho||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\leq CE_{total}.

(101)

This is one of the most important properties that is necessary for the global existence proof. Now we will obtain a differential equation for the total energy. The time derivative of the total energy reads

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{total}}{dt}=\frac{dE_{Ein}}{dt}+\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt}.

(102)

Here the gravitational part
\frac{dE_{Ein}}{dt}
can be evaluated the same way as in
[
28
]
and only the matter coupling terms need to be kept track of. The delicate part is the computation of the time derivative of the fluid energy. Therefore we focus on evaluating
\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt}
first. The following lemma yields a differential equation for the fluid energy.

Lemma 3:

Let the adiabatic index
\gamma_{ad}
lie in the interval
(1,\frac{n+1}{n})
and
a(t)
,
t\in(0,\infty)
be the scale factor. Assume
(u,k^{tr},\dot{\rho},\dot{v})
solve the Einstein’s and commuted Euler’s equations (
50
-
2.2
). Then the fluid energy
E_{fluid}:=-\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{C}^{\mu}\mathbf{n}_{\mu}\mu_{g}
satisfies the following differential equation in time

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt}=\frac{n\dot{a}}{a}\int_{\Sigma}N(N-1)\mathcal{C}^{0}\mu_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}+(\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}X^{i}+\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}N)\mathcal{C}^{0}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{aN}\mathcal{C}^{i}\nabla_{i}N\right)N\mu_{g}.

Proof:
The proof is a consequence of an elementary divergence identity for the energy current
\mathcal{C}
. Integrating the divergence identity (on the scale-free geometry)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\mathcal{C}^{\mu}=\frac{1}{N\mu_{g}}\partial_{\mu}(N\mu_{g}\mathcal{C}^{\mu})

(103)

over a Cauchy hypersurface
\Sigma
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\nabla_{\mu}\mathcal{C}^{\mu}N\mu_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{\mu}(N\mu_{g}\mathcal{C}^{\mu})=\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}(N\mu_{g}\mathcal{C}^{0})+\frac{1}{a}\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{i}(N\mu_{g}\mathcal{C}^{i})

(104)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}N\mathcal{C}^{0}\mu_{g}=-\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Sigma_{t}}\mathcal{C}^{\mu}\mathbf{n}_{\mu}\mu_{g}=\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt},

where we have used Stokes’s theorem on closed manifold to remove the total derivative term.
Explicit computation of
\nabla_{\mu}\mathcal{C}^{\mu}
yields the following differential equation for the fluid energy
E_{fluid}

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt}=\frac{n\dot{a}}{a}\int_{\Sigma}N(N-1)\mathcal{C}^{0}\mu_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}+(\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}X^{i}+\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}N)\mathcal{C}^{0}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{aN}\mathcal{C}^{i}\nabla_{i}N\right)N\mu_{g}.~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Box

Notice that this energy equation contains the top order term
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}
which from the definition of the energy current, will consist of terms that are derivatives of the commuted variables
\dot{\rho}
and
\dot{v}^{i}
. This is dangerous since the energy does not contain any differentiated
\dot{\rho}
and
\dot{v}^{i}
terms. However, by using explicit computations, we will show that such dangerous terms cancel point-wise with their negative counter-parts or yield terms that are of total derivative forms and therefore vanish by integration. This cancellation property exhibits the hyperbolic nature of Euler’s equations.

Claim:

The entity
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}
contains purely algebraic terms of
\dot{\rho}
and
\dot{v}
modulo terms involving Lie derivatives with respect to the shift vector field
X
.

Proof:
We compute the term
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}
explicitly. First we compute
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}
and
\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}
separately. We write the following

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}=[\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}]_{principal}+[\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}]_{remainder},

(105)

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}=[\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}]_{principal}+[\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}]_{remainder},

(106)

where the subscript
principal
denotes the terms that involve highest derivatives of
\dot{\rho}
and
\dot{v}
and
remainder
denotes the terms that are purely algebraic in
(\dot{\rho},\dot{v})
. Explicit calculation yields

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}]_{principal}=\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\left(\frac{\dot{\rho}g_{ij}v^{i}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\partial_{t}\dot{v}^{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{g_{ij}v^{i}\dot{v}^{j}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}\right)+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left[(2g_{ij}(\partial_{t}\dot{v}^{i})\dot{v}^{j}-\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})g_{ij}v^{i}\partial_{t}\dot{v}^{j}}{1+g(v,v)}\right],

\displaystyle[\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}]_{principal}=\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}\dot{\rho}v^{i}\nabla_{i}\dot{\rho}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\left[\dot{\rho}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{i}+\dot{v}^{i}\nabla_{i}\dot{\rho}\right]

(107)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{ad}\rho v^{i}\left[2\dot{v}_{k}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{k}-\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})g(v,\nabla_{i}\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}\right],

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}]_{remainder}

(108)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}}\left(\frac{[1+g(v,v)]^{-1/2}}{2\rho N}(\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v))-\frac{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\rho^{2}N^{2}}[\rho\partial_{t}N+N\partial_{t}\rho]\right)\dot{\rho}^{2}

\displaystyle+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\dot{\rho}\dot{v}^{j}\left(-\frac{\partial_{t}Ng_{ij}v^{i}}{N^{2}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}-\frac{g_{ij}v^{i}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{3/2}}[\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v)]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}(\partial_{t}g_{ij}v^{i}+g_{ij}\partial_{t}v^{i})\right)+\gamma_{ad}\left[g(\dot{v},\dot{v})-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}\right]\left(\frac{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\partial_{t}\rho\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{-1/2}}{2N}(\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v))-\frac{\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N^{2}}\partial_{t}N\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left[(\partial_{t}g_{ij})\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}+\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{[1+g(v,v)]^{2}}(\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v))\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}(\partial_{t}g_{ij}v^{i}\dot{v}^{j}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\partial_{t}v^{j})\right],

\displaystyle[\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}]_{remainder}

(109)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}}\frac{\dot{\rho}^{2}}{\rho^{2}}v^{i}\nabla_{i}\rho+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}}\frac{\dot{\rho}^{2}}{\rho}\nabla_{i}v^{i}+\gamma_{ad}[v^{i}\nabla_{i}\rho+\rho\nabla_{i}v^{i}]

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g(\dot{v},\dot{v})\right]+\gamma_{ad}\rho v^{i}\left(\frac{2g(v,\nabla_{i}v)g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{[1+g(v,v)]^{2}}-\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})g(\dot{v},\nabla_{i}v)}{1+g(v,v)}\right).

Now notice that there are several terms involving derivatives of the commuted fluid variables
(\dot{\rho},\dot{v})
in the expressions of
[\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}]_{principal}
and
[\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}]_{principal}
that appear in a complicated fashion. Therefore we need to carefully combine these terms to obtain cancellation of the dangerous terms. Using the commuted equation (
80
), we expand the first term
\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}
of
[\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}]_{principal}
as follows

\displaystyle\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}

(110)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}\left(-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho N\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{i}}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{a}L_{X}\dot{\rho}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho g(\partial_{t}\dot{v},v)}{1+g(v,v)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{NL_{v}\dot{\rho}}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho v_{i}L_{X}\dot{v}^{i}}{a[1+g(v,v)]}+\mathcal{T}_{1}\right).

Now collection of the principal terms together with the previous expression yields

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}\right)_{principal}

(111)

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}\dot{\rho}\left(-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho N\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{i}}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{a}L_{X}\dot{\rho}-\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho g(\partial_{t}\dot{v},v)}{1+g(v,v)}}_{B}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{NL_{v}\dot{\rho}}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho v_{i}L_{X}\dot{v}^{i}}{a[1+g(v,v)]}+\mathcal{T}_{1}\right)+\underbrace{\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\dot{\rho}g(v,\partial_{t}\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}}_{-B}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)g(v,\dot{v})\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}g(\dot{v},\partial_{t}\dot{v})-\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho g(v,\dot{v})g(v,\partial_{t}\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}}_{1A:~Combine~and~use~equations~of~variations}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{a}\left(\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}\dot{\rho}v^{i}\nabla_{i}\dot{\rho}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)[\dot{\rho}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{i}+\dot{v}^{i}\nabla_{i}\dot{\rho}]+\gamma_{ad}\rho v^{i}[2\dot{v}_{k}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})g(v,\nabla_{i}\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}]\right).

We have numbered the terms here so that we can keep track of each dangerous term and how they cancel with their negative counterpart. First notice that the terms denoted by
B
and
-B
cancel each other (we denote them accordingly). Now we consider the combination of three terms denoted by
1A

\displaystyle 1A=\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)g(v,\dot{v})\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}g(\dot{v},\partial_{t}\dot{v})-\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho g(v,\dot{v})g(v,\partial_{t}\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left((\gamma_{ad}-1)\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}-\gamma_{ad}\rho g(v,\partial_{t}\dot{v})\right)+\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}g(\dot{v},\partial_{t}\dot{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left((\gamma_{ad}-1)\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}-g(v,\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}L_{X}\dot{v}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}\nabla\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}vL_{v}\dot{\rho}-(\gamma_{ad}-1)v\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}vL_{X}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}g\left(\dot{v},\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}L_{X}\dot{v}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}\nabla\dot{\rho}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}vL_{v}\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(\gamma_{ad}-1)v\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}vL_{X}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left((\gamma_{ad}-1)\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}(1+g(v,v))\right)-\frac{2[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}(\gamma_{ad}-1)g(v,\dot{v})\partial_{t}\dot{\rho}}_{point-wise~cancellation}

\displaystyle+\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left(-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}g(v,L_{X}\dot{v})+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}g(v,v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v})+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}L_{v}\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}g(v,v)L_{v}\dot{\rho}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}g(v,v)L_{X}\dot{\rho}-g(v,\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left(\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}g(\dot{v},L_{X}\dot{v})-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}g(\dot{v},v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v})-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}g(v,\dot{v})L_{v}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}g(v,\dot{v})L_{X}\dot{\rho}+g(\dot{v},\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)+\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}\dot{\rho}\mathcal{T}_{1},

\displaystyle=\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left(-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}g(v,L_{X}\dot{v})+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}g(v,v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v})+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}L_{v}\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}g(v,v)L_{v}\dot{\rho}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}g(v,v)L_{X}\dot{\rho}-g(v,\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left(\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}g(\dot{v},L_{X}\dot{v})-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}g(\dot{v},v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v})-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{\rho}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}g(v,\dot{v})L_{v}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}g(v,\dot{v})L_{X}\dot{\rho}+g(\dot{v},\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)+\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}\dot{\rho}\mathcal{T}_{1}.

Substitution of
1A
in the expression of
(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i})_{principal}
(after carrying out the possible point-wise cancellations) yields

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i})_{principal}=\underbrace{-\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{a}\dot{\rho}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{i}}_{I}+\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)(1+g(v,v))^{1/2}}{a\gamma_{ad}\rho N}\dot{\rho}L_{X}\dot{\rho}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{a\gamma_{ad}\rho}\dot{\rho}L_{v}\dot{\rho}}_{II}+\underbrace{\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{aN[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\dot{\rho}g(v,L_{X}\dot{v})}_{III}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{a}\left(\underbrace{\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{\gamma_{ad}\rho}\dot{\rho}v^{i}\nabla_{i}\dot{\rho}}_{-II}+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)[\underbrace{\dot{\rho}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{i}}_{-I}+\underbrace{\dot{v}^{i}\nabla_{i}\dot{\rho}}_{IV}]+\gamma_{ad}\rho v^{i}[\underbrace{2\dot{v}_{k}\nabla_{i}\dot{v}^{k}}_{V}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\underbrace{\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})g(v,\nabla_{i}\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}]}_{VI}\right)+\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left(-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}g(v,L_{X}\dot{v})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}g(v,v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v})}_{-VI}+\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}L_{v}\dot{\rho}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}g(v,v)L_{v}\dot{\rho}}_{VII}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}g(v,v)L_{X}\dot{\rho}}_{III}-g(v,\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left(\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{a}g(\dot{v},L_{X}\dot{v})-\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{au^{0}}g(\dot{v},v^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v})}_{-V}-\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{\rho}}_{-IV}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{au^{0}}g(v,\dot{v})L_{v}\dot{\rho}}_{-VII}+\underbrace{\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a}g(v,\dot{v})L_{X}\dot{\rho}}_{III}+g(\dot{v},\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}\dot{\rho}\mathcal{T}_{1}.

Here the terms that are denoted by the same number belong to the same category, that is, the combination of these terms yields lower-order innocuous terms. The remaining terms are denoted by numbers and their negative counterparts are denoted by the negative of their numbers. Note that there are three terms that fall under the category
III
that requires the following few lines of additional calculations to generate lower-order innocuous terms

\displaystyle\dot{\rho}g(v,L_{X}\dot{v})=\dot{\rho}g(v,X^{k}\nabla_{k}\dot{v}-\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}X)

(112)

\displaystyle=\dot{\rho}X^{k}\nabla_{k}g(v,\dot{v})-\dot{\rho}g(X^{k}\nabla_{k}v,\dot{v})-\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}X)

\displaystyle=X^{k}\nabla_{k}(\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}))-g(v,\dot{v})L_{X}\dot{\rho}-\dot{\rho}g(X^{k}\nabla_{k}v,\dot{v})-\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}X)

\displaystyle=-g(v,\dot{v})L_{X}\dot{\rho}-L_{X}(\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}))-\dot{\rho}g(X^{k}\nabla_{k}v,\dot{v})-\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}X).

After all the possible cancellations, we collect the remaining terms to obtain the following final expression for
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i})N\mu_{g}

(113)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left(\underbrace{\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)(1+g(v,v))^{1/2}}{a\gamma_{ad}\rho N}\dot{\rho}L_{X}\dot{\rho}}_{I}-\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho g(v,\dot{v})}{aN[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}g(v,L_{X}\dot{v})}_{II}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}g(v,\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})+\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{aN}g(\dot{v},L_{X}\dot{v})}_{III}+\frac{2[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}g(\dot{v},\frac{\mathcal{T}_{2}}{u^{0}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)}{aN[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}\left(\underbrace{L_{X}(\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}))}_{IV}+\dot{\rho}g(X^{k}\nabla_{k}v,\dot{v})+\dot{\rho}g(v,\dot{v}^{k}\nabla_{k}X)\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2(\gamma_{ad}-1)[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}\dot{\rho}\mathcal{T}_{1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}}\left(\frac{[1+g(v,v)]^{-1/2}}{2\rho N}(\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v))-\frac{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{\rho^{2}N^{2}}[\rho\partial_{t}N+N\partial_{t}\rho]\right)\dot{\rho}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2(\gamma_{ad}-1)\dot{\rho}\dot{v}^{j}\left(-\frac{\partial_{t}Ng_{ij}v^{i}}{N^{2}[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}-\frac{g_{ij}v^{i}}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{3/2}}[\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v)]\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{1}{N[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}(\partial_{t}g_{ij}v^{i}+g_{ij}\partial_{t}v^{i})\right)+\gamma_{ad}\left[g(\dot{v},\dot{v})-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}\right]\left(\frac{[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\partial_{t}\rho\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{-1/2}}{2N}(\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v))-\frac{\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N^{2}}\partial_{t}N\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}{N}\left[(\partial_{t}g_{ij})\dot{v}^{i}\dot{v}^{j}+\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{[1+g(v,v)]^{2}}(\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})}{1+g(v,v)}(\partial_{t}g_{ij}v^{i}\dot{v}^{j}+g_{ij}\dot{v}^{i}\partial_{t}v^{j})\right]+\frac{1}{a}\left(-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}}\frac{\dot{\rho}^{2}}{\rho^{2}}v^{i}\nabla_{i}\rho+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{\gamma_{ad}}\frac{\dot{\rho}^{2}}{\rho}\nabla_{i}v^{i}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\gamma_{ad}[v^{i}\nabla_{i}\rho+\rho\nabla_{i}v^{i}]\left[-\frac{g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{1+g(v,v)}+g(\dot{v},\dot{v})\right]+\gamma_{ad}\rho v^{i}\left(\frac{2g(v,\nabla_{i}v)g(v,\dot{v})^{2}}{[1+g(v,v)]^{2}}\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.-\frac{2g(v,\dot{v})g(\dot{v},\nabla_{i}v)}{1+g(v,v)}\right)\right)\right)N\mu_{g}.

The terms that are numbered
I,II,III
, and
IV
contain derivatives of
\dot{\rho}
and
\dot{v}
. However, these are innocuous since these derivatives involve Lie derivatives with respect to the shift vector field
X
. We will reduce these terms to lower order terms through an integration by parts operation which will be discussed in the next section. The remaining terms are purely algebraic in nature. This completes the proof of the claim. Recall from equation (
102
) and lemma 3 that the time derivative of the total energy reads

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{total}}{dt}=\frac{dE_{Ein}}{dt}+\int_{\Sigma}\left(\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}+(\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}X^{i}+\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t}N)\mathcal{C}^{0}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{aN}\mathcal{C}^{i}\nabla_{i}N\right)N\mu_{g}+\frac{n\dot{a}}{a}\int_{\Sigma}N(N-1)\mathcal{C}^{0}\mu_{g}.

Using the reduced expression for
\partial_{t}\mathcal{C}^{0}+\frac{1}{a}\nabla_{i}\mathcal{C}^{i}
(
113
), we will estimate
\frac{dE_{total}}{dt}
.

4
Energy estimates

In this section, we will obtain a differential inequality for the total energy. For this purpose, we will need the following set of inequalities.

4.1
Important inequalities

1.

[
69
]

Let
f_{1}\in H^{t_{1}},~f_{2}\in H^{t_{2}}
and
k\leq\min(t_{1},t_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}-\frac{n}{2}),~t_{i}\geq 0
and some
t_{i}>0
, then the following estimate holds

\displaystyle||f_{1}f_{2}||_{H^{k}}\leq C||f_{1}||_{H^{t_{1}}}||f_{2}||_{H^{t_{2}}}

(114)

2.

[
70
]

For
\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{OP}^{s},~s\in\mathbb{R}
, the following holds

\displaystyle||\mathcal{P}f||_{H^{r}}\leq C||f||_{H^{s+r}},~~r\in\mathbb{R}.

(115)

Here
\mathcal{OP}^{s}
denotes the space of pseudo-differential operators with symbol in Hormander’s class
S^{s}_{1,0}
(see
[
70
]
). For example
\nabla[\gamma]\in\mathcal{OP}^{1}
.

3.
[
70
]

Assume
\mathcal{P}\in\mathcal{OP}^{s},~s>0
, then

\displaystyle||[P,f_{1}]f_{2}||_{H^{\sigma}}\leq C\left(||\nabla[\gamma]f_{1}||_{L^{\infty}}||f_{2}||_{H^{s-1+\sigma}}+||f_{1}||_{H^{s+\sigma}}||f_{2}||_{L^{\infty}}\right)

(116)

4.
[
70
]

Let
1\leq s<\mu
and
F\in C^{\mu}(\mathbb{R})
with
F(0)=0
, where
C^{\mu}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the Hölder space. Then for
f\in H^{s}\cap L^{\infty}
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle||F(f)||_{H^{s}}\leq C||f||_{H^{s}}(1+||f||^{\mu-1}_{L^{\infty}})

(117)

5.
[
71
]

If
s>0
, then
H^{s}\cap L^{\infty}
is an algebra i.e.,

\displaystyle||f_{1}f_{2}||_{H^{s}}\leq C(||f_{1}||_{L^{\infty}}||f_{2}||_{H^{s}}+||f_{1}||_{H^{s}}||f_{2}||_{L^{\infty}})

(118)

and for
s>\frac{n}{2}+1

\displaystyle||f_{1}f_{2}||_{H^{s}}\leq C||f_{1}||_{H^{s}}||f_{2}||_{H^{s}}

(119)

due to Sobolev embedding.

In addition to these inequalities, we will also use Hölder’s and Minkowski’s inequality whenever necessary. First we obtain the necessary elliptic estimates

4.2
Elliptic estimates

In this section, we will obtain estimates for the perturbation to the lapse function
\omega=N-1
, the shift vector field
X
, and the shifted shift vector field
Y^{||}+\frac{1}{a}X
. These estimates will be obtained by utilizing the elliptic equations that are obtained by means of gauge fixing. Before moving to the analysis of the elliptic equations, we need to obtain an estimate for
\partial_{t}\gamma
describing the motion parallel to the center manifold
\mathcal{N}
. The following lemma provides an estimate for
\partial_{t}\gamma

Lemma 4:

Let
\gamma\in\mathcal{N}
be the shadow of g such that
||g-\gamma||_{H^{s}}<\delta
for a suitable
\delta>0
,
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
. The
\mathcal{N}-
tangential velocity
\partial_{t}\gamma
verifies the following estimate

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}

(120)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}\right),

for a constant
C
that depends on a fixed background geometry.

Proof:
In the light of the shadow gauge condition (
72
), the
\mathcal{N}-
tangential velocity
\partial_{t}\gamma
satisfies

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}\gamma||\leq C||\partial_{t}g||_{H^{s-1}},

(121)

where the norm in the left hand side refers to a Sobolev norm of arbitrary order (since
\partial_{t}\gamma
is an element of a finite dimensional vector space). Utilizing the evolution equation (
50
) and Minkowski’s inequality together with the fact that
H^{s_{1}}\hookrightarrow H^{s_{2}}
for
s_{1}>s_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}\gamma||

(122)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}\right),

and in particular

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}

(123)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}\right)

with a possible modification of the constant
C
.
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Box

Now we will obtain the estimates for
\omega
,
\partial_{t}N
and
X
utilizing the elliptic equations obtained through gauge fixing. The following lemma provides an estimate for the perturbation to the elapse function.

Lemma 5:

Let
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
and
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
system in CMCSH gauge. Then the following estimate holds for the perturbation to the lapse function (
\omega=N-1
)

\displaystyle||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\leq

(124)

\displaystyle C(||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})).

Proof:
The elliptic equation for the perturbation
\omega=N-1
to the lapse function
N
reads

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}\omega+\underbrace{\left(|k^{tr}|^{2}+\frac{2C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}[\frac{(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}+\gamma_{ad}g(v,v)](C_{\rho}+\delta\rho)\right)}_{>0}\omega

\displaystyle=-|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}-\frac{n\gamma_{ad}-2}{n-1}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\delta\rho-\gamma_{ad}C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}g(v,v)-\gamma_{ad}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}g(v,v)\delta\rho.

Now the elliptic operator

\Delta_{g}+\left(|k^{tr}|^{2}+\frac{2C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}[\frac{(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}+\gamma_{ad}g(v,v)](C_{\rho}+\delta\rho)\right)id:H^{s+1}\to H^{s-1}
is an isomorphism yielding the following estimate

\displaystyle||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\leq

(125)

\displaystyle C(||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})).

A short proof by contradiction may be as follows. Let us denote the elliptic operator in question by
\mathcal{Q}
. Assume that an estimate of the type
||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\lesssim||\mathcal{Q}\omega||_{H^{s-1}}
does not hold. Then there exists a sequence
\{\omega_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
with
||\omega_{i}||_{H^{s+1}}=1
and
||\mathcal{Q}\omega_{i}||_{H^{s-1}}\to 0
as
i\to\infty
. Now
\Sigma
is compact and therefore
H^{s+1}
is compactly embedded into
H^{s-1}
(notice
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
). This yields a sub-sequence
\{\omega_{i_{j}}\}
converging to
\omega^{*}\in H^{s+1}
strongly in
H^{s-1}
, which by construction satisfies
\mathcal{Q}\omega^{*}=0
. This contradicts the fact that the operator
\Omega
is injective. Therefore an estimate of the type
||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\lesssim||A\omega||_{H^{s-1}}
holds
\forall s\geq 1
(which is satisfied here since
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
).

Estimation of the shift vector field is slightly more involved since the CMCSH gauge is known to exhibit the so called
Gribov
degeneracy
[
72
,
73
]
. The shift equation reads

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}X^{i}-R^{i}_{j}X^{j}=-n(1-\frac{2}{n})\dot{a}\nabla^{i}N-2\nabla^{j}Nk^{tri}_{j}-2N\nabla_{j}k^{trij}

(126)

\displaystyle+(2Nk^{trjk}-2\nabla^{j}X^{k})\left(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk}\right)-ag^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]_{ij}^{k}.

In order to estimate
X
in terms of the remaining variables, we want to show that the map
X\mapsto\Delta_{g}X^{i}-R^{i}_{j}X^{j}+2\nabla^{j}X^{k}\left(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk}\right)
is an isomorphism. However, apart from the operator
\Delta_{g}
, the remaining two terms do not have a definite sign. Therefore, it is not obvious that such an isomorphism property holds. However, here we will explicitly prove the isomorphism property of this operator using the small data assumption (
||g-\gamma||_{H^{s}}<\delta
). The following lemma provides the necessary estimate for the shift vector field.

Lemma 6:

Let
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
and
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
system in CMCSH gauge. Then the following estimate holds for the shift vector field

\displaystyle||X||_{H^{s+1}}\leq C\left(\dot{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\right.

(127)

\displaystyle\left.\{||v||^{3}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+||v||_{H^{s-1}}\}+||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\dot{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right)

Proof:
The first step in obtaining the estimate for
X
is to prove that the map
X\mapsto\Delta_{g}X^{i}-R^{i}_{j}X^{j}+2\nabla^{j}X^{k}\left(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk}\right)
is an isomorphism between suitable function spaces. Let us denote the flow of
X
on a constant time hypersurface by
\varphi_{s}
(
s
being the flow parameter). Let us pull back
V^{\flat}
(where
V^{i}:=g^{kl}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{kl}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{kl})
is the negative of the tension vector field) by the flow of
X
to obtain the following

\displaystyle[(\varphi^{*}_{s}V^{\flat})^{\sharp}]^{i}=(\varphi^{*}_{s}g)^{jk}(\varphi^{*}_{s}(\Gamma[g]-\Gamma[\gamma]))^{i}_{kl}=(\varphi^{*}_{s}g)^{jk}((\Gamma[\varphi^{*}_{s}g]-\Gamma[\varphi^{*}_{s}\gamma]))^{i}_{kl}

which holds because of the fact that the difference of connections transforms as a section of a suitable vector bundle. Differentiating both sides with respect to
s
and setting
s=0
yields

\displaystyle L_{X}V^{i}=(L_{X}g)^{jk}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk})+g^{jk}\frac{d}{ds}\left((\Gamma[\varphi^{*}_{s}g]-\Gamma[\varphi^{*}_{s}\gamma])^{i}_{jk}\right)|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=(L_{X}g)^{jk}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk})+\frac{1}{2}g^{jk}g^{il}\left[\nabla_{j}(L_{X}g_{lk})+\nabla_{k}(L_{X}g_{jl})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\nabla_{l}(L_{X}g_{jk})\right]-\frac{1}{2}g^{jk}\gamma^{il}\left[\nabla[\gamma]_{j}(L_{X}\gamma_{lk})+\nabla[\gamma]_{k}(L_{X}\gamma_{jl})-\nabla[\gamma]_{l}(L_{X}\gamma_{jk})\right]

Explicit computation in CMCSH gauge (
V^{i}=0
) yields

\displaystyle g^{kl}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{l}X^{i}+R^{i}_{j}X^{j}+2\nabla^{k}X^{l}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{kl}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{kl})

(128)

\displaystyle=g^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}X^{i}_{\gamma}+g^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}X^{m}_{\gamma},

where
X^{i}_{\gamma}:=X_{j}\gamma^{ij}
. Now the map
P:X\mapsto-g^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}X^{i}_{\gamma}-g^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}X^{m}_{\gamma}
is an isomorphism which may be shown as follows. Let’s consider that
\ker(P)
is not empty and
0\neq Z\in\ker(P)
i.e.,

\displaystyle-g^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Z^{i}-g^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}Z^{m}=0.

(129)

Multiplying both sides of the above equation by
Z
and integrating over
\Sigma
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(-g^{kl}Z_{i}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Z^{i}-g^{kl}Z_{i}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}Z^{m}\right)\mu_{\gamma}=0

(130)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}\left(\gamma^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}Z_{i}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Z^{i}-\gamma^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mkil}Z^{i}Z^{m}-(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})Z_{i}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Z^{i}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})R[\gamma]_{mkil}Z^{i}Z^{m}\right)\mu_{\gamma}=0

Now for
||g-\gamma||_{H^{s}}<\delta
sufficiently small, we have

\displaystyle\left(\gamma^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}Z_{i}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Z^{i}-\gamma^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mkil}Z^{i}Z^{m}-(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})Z_{i}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Z^{i}\right.

(131)

\displaystyle\left.-(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})R[\gamma]_{mkil}Z^{i}Z^{m}\right)>0

since
Ricci[\gamma](Z,Z)<0
yielding

\displaystyle Z=0

(132)

i.e.,
\ker(P)=\{0\}
. Since
\Sigma
is compact and
P
is second order elliptic, showing injectivity is enough to ensure its isomorphism property. Therefore in CMCSH gauge, we establish the isomorphism of the map
X\mapsto g^{kl}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{l}X^{i}+R^{i}_{j}X^{j}+2\nabla^{k}X^{l}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{kl}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{kl})
. Using the equation for the shift (
126
), this isomorphism yields the following estimate for the shift vector field

\displaystyle||X||_{H^{s+1}}\leq C||\left((1-\frac{2}{n})a\tau\nabla^{i}N-2\nabla^{j}Nk^{tri}_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2N\left(\gamma_{ad}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\rho(Nu^{0}-1)v^{i}+\gamma_{ad}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\rho v^{i}\right)+2Nk^{trjk}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk})\right.

\displaystyle r\left.-ag^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]_{ij}^{k}\right)||_{H^{s-1}}.

(133)

The source term that is present in the right-hand side of the previous estimate (
4.2
) contains several terms that are multiplied by
\dot{a}
which may be potentially dangerous. We need to keep track of these terms carefully. The most problematic term
ag^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]_{ij}^{k}
needs to be estimated (notice that it is multiplied by
a
). Since
\partial_{t}\gamma
is tangent to
\mathcal{N}
, it may be written as follows

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-h^{TT||}-L_{Y^{||}}\gamma,

(134)

with
Y^{||}
satisfying (
2.3
)

\displaystyle[\nabla[\gamma]^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}Y^{||i}+R[\gamma]^{||i}_{m}Y^{||m}]-(h^{TT||}+L_{Y^{||}}\gamma)^{mn}(\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}

(135)

\displaystyle-\Gamma[\gamma^{*}]^{i}_{mn})=0

and
h^{TT||}\in\ker(\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma})
.
Now
\gamma^{mn}\partial_{t}\Gamma^{i}_{mn}[\gamma]=\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma^{i}_{mn}[\gamma]\cdot\partial_{t}\gamma
for
\partial_{t}\gamma\in T_{\gamma}\mathcal{N}
may be written as

\displaystyle\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\cdot\partial_{t}\gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{mn}\gamma^{ik}\left(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\partial_{t}\gamma_{kn}+\nabla[\gamma]_{n}\partial_{t}\gamma_{mk}-\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\partial_{t}\gamma_{mn}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\gamma^{ik}\nabla[\gamma]^{n}\partial_{t}\gamma_{kn}-\nabla[\gamma]^{i}(tr_{\gamma}\partial_{t}\gamma),

from which
\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma^{i}_{mn}[\gamma]h^{TT||}=0
follows immediately since
\nabla[\gamma]^{i}h^{TT||}_{ij}=0,~\tr_{\gamma}h^{TT||}=0
. The remaining terms lead to the following

\displaystyle-\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\cdot\partial_{t}\gamma=2\gamma^{ik}\nabla[\gamma]^{n}\left(\nabla[\gamma]_{k}Y^{||}_{n}+\nabla[\gamma]_{n}Y^{||}_{k}\right)-2\nabla[\gamma]^{i}(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}Y^{||m})

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{ik}(\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}Y^{n}+R[\gamma]_{mk}Y^{||m})+2\nabla[\gamma]^{n}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}Y^{||i}-2\nabla[\gamma]^{i}(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}Y^{||m})

\displaystyle=2(\nabla[\gamma]^{n}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}Y^{||i}+R[\gamma]^{i}_{m}Y^{||m})

\displaystyle=2(L_{Y^{||}}\gamma+h^{TT})^{mn}(\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}-\Gamma[\gamma^{*}]^{i}_{mn})

\displaystyle=2(\partial_{t}\gamma)^{mn}(\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}-\Gamma[\gamma^{*}]^{i}_{mn}).

This yields

\displaystyle||\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}\leq C||\gamma-\gamma^{*}||_{H^{s}}||\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}

(136)

Therefore, using the triangle inequality, we have the following

\displaystyle||g^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}\leq C(||g-\gamma||_{H^{s}}+||\gamma-\gamma^{*}||_{H^{s}})||\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}.

(137)

Utilizing the estimate of
\partial_{t}\gamma
from lemma 4, we obtain the following estimate for the shift vector field
X

\displaystyle||X||_{H^{s+1}}\left(1-C(||u||_{H^{s}}+||\gamma-\gamma^{*}||_{H^{s}})(1+||u||_{H^{s}})\right)

(138)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\dot{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.(||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||v||_{H^{s-1}})+||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\dot{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right)

which for sufficiently small
||u||_{H^{s}}+||\gamma-\gamma^{*}||_{H^{s}}
yields

\displaystyle||X||_{H^{s+1}}\leq C\left(\dot{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.(||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||v||_{H^{s-1}})+||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(\dot{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right).~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\Box

Here several terms contain the factor
\dot{a}
which may be potentially dangerous. However, the structure of Einstein’s equations is such that these potentially problematic terms are always multiplied by
\frac{1}{a}
in the energy estimate thereby turning these into innocuous terms. We will discover this fact in a later section where we study the energy estimates.

It is worth mentioning that the smallness of
u:=g-\gamma
is extremely important in the proof of the isomorphism property of
P
. The size of the Gribov domain is essentially defined by the largest value of
||g-\gamma||_{H^{s}}
for which the isomorphism property of
P
holds. If
g
leaves the Gribov horizon, then the CMCSH gauge orbit develops a direction (or directions) of tangency to the gauge slice introducing degeneracy
[
72
,
73
]
. However, in the current context of stability considerations, such a problem does not arise due to the smallness of the data.

In addition to estimating the shift vector field
X
, we note that a new vector field
Y^{||}+\frac{1}{a}X
enters into the field equations (
76
). In order to estimate this new vector field, we will have to perform a series of calculations. The following lemma provides an estimate for
Y^{||}+\frac{1}{a}X

Lemma 7:

Let
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1},~s>\frac{n}{2}+2
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
and
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
system in CMCSH gauge. Then the following estimate holds for the vector field
Y^{||}+\frac{1}{a}X

\displaystyle||Y^{||}+\frac{X}{a}||_{H^{s+1}}\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})(||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||v||^{3}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}}))\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||u||_{H^{s}}||X||_{H^{s+1}}+||u||_{H^{s}}||\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}\right).

Proof:
Let us recall that the
\mathcal{N}-
tangential velocity
\partial_{t}\gamma
verifies
\partial_{t}\gamma=-h^{TT||}-L_{Y^{||}}\gamma
and an explicit calculation shows that the following holds

\displaystyle\gamma^{mn}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}=\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\cdot\partial_{t}\gamma=-\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\cdot(h^{TT||}+L_{Y^{||}}\gamma)

(139)

\displaystyle=-\gamma^{mn}D\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}\cdot L_{Y^{||}}\gamma=-\gamma^{mn}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}Y^{||i}-R[\gamma]^{i}_{j}Y^{||j}.

The calculations from the previous lemma yield

\displaystyle g^{kl}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{l}X^{i}+R^{i}_{j}X^{j}+2\nabla^{k}X^{l}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{kl}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{kl})

(140)

\displaystyle=g^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}X^{i}_{\gamma}+g^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}X^{m}_{\gamma}

which by the virtue of the shift equation (
126
) yields

\displaystyle-g^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}\frac{X^{i}_{\gamma}}{a}-g^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}\frac{X^{m}_{\gamma}}{a}=-(1-\frac{2}{n})n\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\nabla^{i}N

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{a}\nabla^{j}Nk^{tri}_{j}-\frac{2}{a}N\nabla_{j}k^{trij}+\frac{2}{a}Nk^{trjk}\left(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk}\right)-g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]_{ij}^{k}

or

\displaystyle-\gamma^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}\frac{X^{i}_{\gamma}}{a}-\gamma^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}\frac{X^{m}_{\gamma}}{a}=-(1-\frac{2}{n})n\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\nabla^{i}N-\frac{2}{a}\nabla^{j}Nk^{tri}_{j}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{a}N\nabla_{j}k^{trij}+\frac{2}{a}Nk^{trjk}\left(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk}\right)-g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]_{ij}^{k}+(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}\frac{X^{i}_{\gamma}}{a}

\displaystyle+(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}\frac{X^{m}_{\gamma}}{a}

which yields after adding
-\gamma^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}Y^{||i}_{\gamma}-\gamma^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}Y^{||m}_{\gamma}
and using equation (
139
)

\displaystyle-\gamma^{kl}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}(\frac{X^{i}_{\gamma}}{a}+Y^{||i})-\gamma^{kl}R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}(\frac{X^{m}_{\gamma}}{a}+Y^{||m})=-(1-\frac{2}{n})n\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\nabla^{i}N

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{a}\nabla^{j}Nk^{tri}_{j}-\frac{2}{a}N\nabla_{j}k^{trij}+\frac{2}{a}Nk^{trjk}\left(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{jk}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{jk}\right)-g^{mn}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]_{mn}^{i}

\displaystyle+(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{l}\frac{X^{i}_{\gamma}}{a}+(g^{kl}-\gamma^{kl})R[\gamma]_{mk}~^{i}~{}_{l}\frac{X^{m}_{\gamma}}{a}

\displaystyle+\gamma^{mn}\partial_{t}\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{mn}

which yields using momentum constraint (
55
) and
H^{s_{1}}\hookrightarrow H^{s_{2}},~s_{1}>s_{2}

\displaystyle||Y^{||}+\frac{X}{a}||_{H^{s+1}}\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})(||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||v||^{3}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}}))\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||u||_{H^{s}}||X||_{H^{s+1}}+||u||_{H^{s}}||\partial_{t}\gamma||_{H^{s-1}}\right).

In addition to the above estimates, we need to estimate
\partial_{t}N
as well since it appears in the time derivative of the fluid energy current (
3
). We commute the vector field
\partial_{t}
with the elliptic equation for the lapse function (
2.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{g}(\partial_{t}N)+\underbrace{\left(|k^{tr}|^{2}+\frac{2C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}[\frac{(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}+\gamma_{ad}g(v,v)]\rho\right)}_{>0}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}N

\displaystyle=-N\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}N(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}\partial_{t}(a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\delta\rho)-\gamma_{ad}N\partial_{t}(a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}g(v,v))

(141)

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{2C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}+C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\frac{(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}\right)(N-1)+\partial_{t}g^{ij}\nabla[g]_{i}\nabla_{j}N

\displaystyle-g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[g]^{k}_{ij}\nabla_{k}N.

The following lemma provides the necessary estimate for
\partial_{t}N
.

Lemma 8:

Let
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
and
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
system in CMCSH gauge. Then the following estimate holds for the time derivative of the lapse function

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}N||_{H^{s}}\leq C\left(||\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}||_{H^{s-2}}+\dot{a}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}||\partial_{t}\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\dot{a}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}(||\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v)||_{H^{s-2}})+\dot{a}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+||\partial_{t}g||_{H^{s-2}}(||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+||u||_{H^{s}}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})+C||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}(||X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+(1+||u||_{H^{s}})||X||_{H^{s+1}})\right)\right)

Proof:
Elliptic regularity theory when applied to the equation for the time derivative of the lapse function (
4.2
) yields

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}N||_{H^{s}}

(142)

\displaystyle\leq C||-N\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}-\frac{\gamma_{ad}N(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}\partial_{t}(a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\delta\rho)-\gamma_{ad}N\partial_{t}(a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}g(v,v))

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}\left(\frac{2C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}}{n-1}+\frac{1}{n-1}+C_{\rho}a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}\frac{(n\gamma_{ad}-2)}{n-1}\right)(N-1)+\partial_{t}g^{ij}\nabla[g]_{i}\nabla_{j}N

\displaystyle-g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[g]^{k}_{ij}\nabla_{k}N||_{H^{s-2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(||\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}||_{H^{s-2}}+\dot{a}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}}+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}||\partial_{t}\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}}+\dot{a}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a^{2-n\gamma_{ad}}(||\partial_{t}g(v,v)+2g(\partial_{t}v,v)||_{H^{s-2}})+\dot{a}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||\omega||_{H^{s-2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+||\partial_{t}g||_{H^{s-2}}(||\omega||_{H^{s}}+||u||_{H^{s-1}}||\omega||_{H^{s-1}})+||g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[g]^{k}_{ij}\nabla_{k}N||_{H^{s-2}}\right).

Now we need to estimate the last term i.e.,
||g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[g]^{k}_{ij}\nabla_{k}N||_{H^{s-2}}
. We do so in the following way. Explicit calculations yield

\displaystyle||g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[g]^{k}_{ij}\nabla_{k}N||_{H^{s-2}}

(143)

\displaystyle=||g^{ij}g^{kl}(\nabla_{i}\partial_{t}g_{lj}+\nabla_{j}\partial_{t}g_{il}-\nabla_{l}\partial_{t}g_{ij})\nabla_{k}N||_{H^{s-2}}

Now before applying the algebra property of the Sobolev spaces, we do the following

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\partial_{t}g_{lj}=\nabla[\gamma]_{i}\partial_{t}g_{lj}-(\Gamma[g]^{m}_{il}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{m}_{il})\partial_{t}g_{mj}-(\Gamma[g]^{m}_{ij}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{m}_{ij})\partial_{t}g_{ml}

(144)

and write
(L_{X}g)_{ij}=X^{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}u_{ij}+g_{ik}\nabla[\gamma]_{j}X^{k}+g_{kj}\nabla[\gamma]_{i}X^{k}
. Combining everything, we obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle||g^{ij}\partial_{t}\Gamma[g]^{k}_{ij}\nabla_{k}N||_{H^{s-2}}

(145)

\displaystyle\leq C||N-1||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s-2}})\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||N-1||_{H^{s-2}}(1+||u||_{H^{s-2}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}(1+||N-1||_{H^{s-1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}}+\frac{1}{a}(||X||_{H^{s-2}}||u||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(1+||u||_{H^{s-2}})||X||_{H^{s-1}})\right).

Collecting all the terms and using
H^{s_{1}}\hookrightarrow H^{s_{2}},~s_{1}>s_{2}
, we obtain the result.
\Box

Notice that there are terms such as
||\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}||_{H^{s-2}}
,
||\partial_{t}\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}}
, and
||g(\partial_{t}v,v)||_{H^{s-2}}
estimation of which require the evolution equations. The following two lemmas provide the necessary estimates.

Lemma 9:

Let
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
and
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
. Also let
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
evolution equations. Then
\partial_{t}k^{tr}
and
\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}
verify the following estimates

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}}\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s-2}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}}+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s-2}})||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}(||\omega||_{H^{s-2}}+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s-2}}))\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s}}+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-2}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s-2}})(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}(||X||_{H^{s-2}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+||X||_{H^{s-1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}(||u||_{H^{s}}+||u||^{2}_{H^{s}})(1+||\omega||_{H^{s-2}})\right),

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}||_{H^{s-2}}\leq C\left((\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s-2}}+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s-2}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s-2}}||u||_{H^{s-1}}+||X||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s-2}}))||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-2}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(1+||u||_{H^{s-2}})||\partial_{t}k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-2}}\right).

Proof:
Let us write down the evolution equation for
k^{tr}_{ij}
in the following way

\displaystyle\frac{\partial k^{tr}_{ij}}{\partial t}=-\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(n-1)k^{tr}_{ij}-\frac{1}{a}\nabla[\gamma]_{i}\nabla_{j}\omega+\frac{1}{a}X^{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}k^{tr}_{ij}+\frac{1}{2a}N\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{ij}+\mathcal{F}_{2ij},

where
\mathcal{F}_{2}
is defined as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2ij}=-\frac{2}{a}(1+\omega)k^{tr}_{ik}k^{trk}_{j}+\left(a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}\frac{\omega\gamma_{ad}C_{\rho}+N(\gamma_{ad}-2)\delta\rho}{n-1}+\frac{\omega}{a(n-1)}\right)g_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2a}g^{lk}(\nabla[\gamma]_{i}u_{jk}+\nabla[\gamma]_{j}u_{ik}-\nabla[\gamma]_{k}u_{ij})\nabla_{l}\omega-\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(n-2)\omega k^{tr}_{ij}

(146)

\displaystyle-\gamma_{ad}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+\omega)(C_{\rho}+\delta\rho)v_{i}v_{j}+\frac{1}{a}k^{tr}_{ik}\nabla[\gamma]_{j}X^{k}+\frac{1}{a}k^{tr}_{kj}\nabla[\gamma]_{i}X^{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{a}(1+\omega)\mathfrak{J}_{ij}.

Using product estimate (
118
), other elementary inequalities, and
||\mathfrak{J}||_{H^{s-1}}\leq C||u||^{2}_{H^{s}},
the estimate for
\partial_{t}k^{tr}
follows. For
\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}
, we write the following using symmetry of
k^{tr}
and
g

\displaystyle\partial_{t}|k^{tr}|^{2}_{g}=\partial_{t}(g^{ik}g^{jl}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl})=2\partial_{t}g^{ik}g^{jl}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}+2g^{ik}g^{jl}\partial_{t}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl},

(147)

and use the product estimate (
118
), equation of motion for
g
(
76
), and elementary calculus inequalities to obtain the desired estimate.

Lemma 10:

Let
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
and
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
. Also let
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
evolution equations. Then
\partial_{t}\delta\rho
and
\partial_{t}v
verify the following estimates

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}\delta\rho||_{H^{s-2}}\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})+\frac{1}{a}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\right.

(148)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{3}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{3}_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||X||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}})+\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}||X||_{H^{s+1}}+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}\right),

\displaystyle||\partial_{t}v||_{H^{s-2}}\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})+\frac{1}{a}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

(149)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||X||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}})\right)(1+||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}})

\displaystyle+C||v||_{H^{s-1}}\left(\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}||X||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}\right)

Proof:
In order to estimate
\partial_{t}\delta\rho
and
\partial_{t}v
, we need to obtain their explicit expressions. We do so by using the original evolution equations (
52
-
2.2
) and elementary linear algebra

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\rho=-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho}{1+2g(v,v)-\gamma_{ad}g(v,v)}g(\mathcal{Z}_{2},v)+\frac{1+g(v,v)}{1+2g(v,v)-\gamma_{ad}g(v,v)}\mathcal{Z}_{1},

(150)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v^{i}=\left(\frac{(\gamma_{ad}-1)g(\mathcal{Z}_{2},v)}{1+2g(v,v)-\gamma_{ad}g(v,v)}-\frac{(\gamma_{ad}-1)(1+g(v,v))\mathcal{Z}_{1}}{\gamma_{ad}\rho(1+2g(v,v)-\gamma_{ad}g(v,v))}\right)v^{i}

(151)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{Z}^{i}_{2}.

Here
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{i}_{2}
are defined as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{1}:=-\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho N\nabla_{i}v^{i}}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}-\frac{(N-1)n\gamma_{ad}\rho\dot{a}}{a}+\frac{1}{a}L_{X}\rho

(152)

\displaystyle-\frac{NL_{v}\rho}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}}+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho N}{1+g(v,v)}\left(\frac{1}{a}k^{tr}(v,v)-\frac{\dot{a}}{a}g(v,v)\right)+\frac{\gamma_{ad}\rho v_{i}L_{X}v^{i}}{a[1+g(v,v)]}

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma_{ad}N\rho L_{v}N}{a[1+g(v,v)]^{1/2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{i}_{2}:=-[2N-1-n(\gamma_{ad}-1)]\frac{\dot{a}}{a}v^{i}+\frac{2Nk^{tri}_{j}v^{j}}{a}+\frac{1}{a}L_{X}v^{i}

\displaystyle-\frac{v^{j}\nabla_{j}v^{i}}{au^{0}}-\frac{1+g(v,v)}{aNu^{0}}\nabla^{i}N-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a\gamma_{ad}\rho u^{0}}\nabla^{i}\rho-\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a\gamma_{ad}\rho u^{0}}v^{i}L_{v}\rho+\frac{\gamma_{ad}-1}{a\gamma_{ad}\rho}v^{i}L_{X}\rho.

Now since
\rho=\delta\rho+C_{\rho}
(
C_{\rho}
is a constant), action of derivatives on
\rho
and
\delta\rho
are the same and therefore
\partial_{t}\rho
and
\partial_{t}\delta\rho
will have the same meaning. Using the normalization condition
Nu^{0}=\sqrt{1+g(v,v)}
, the composition estimate (
117
), the product estimate (
118
), and Sobolev embedding, we obtain the following for
\mathcal{Z}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle||\mathcal{Z}_{1}||_{H^{s-2}}\leq C\left(\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}||X||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}\right),

\displaystyle||\mathcal{Z}_{2}||_{H^{s-2}}\leq C\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||v||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})+\frac{1}{a}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

(153)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}(1+||v||_{H^{s-1}}+||X||_{H^{s+1}}||v||_{H^{s-1}})\right),

where we have replaced lower order Sobolev norms by the maximum available ones.
Using the equations (
150
-
151
), the estimates for
\partial_{t}\delta\rho
and
\partial_{t}v
follow.
\Box

4.3
Time evolution of total energy

Utilizing the elliptic estimates obtained in the previous section and the calculations associated with the fluid energy current in section (3), we will explicitly estimate the time derivative of the total energy.
We need to estimate the time evolution of the total energy

E_{total}

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{total}}{dt}=\frac{dE_{Ein}}{dt}+\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt}.

(154)

Since we have already taken care of the principal terms that arise in the expression
\frac{dE_{fluid}}{dt}
, we will focus on on the gravity part instead. Explicit calculations yield

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{Ein}}{dt}

(155)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\sum_{I=1}^{s}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{ij}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{I=1}^{s}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I=1}^{s}\left(\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}\partial_{t}u_{ij})u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}+\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{ij}\partial_{t}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}[\partial_{t},\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}]u_{ij}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}\partial_{t}k^{tr}_{ij})k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}_{ij}\partial_{t}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}[\partial_{t},\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}]k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{8}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{ij}u_{kl}\partial_{t}(\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl})\mu_{g}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}\partial_{t}(\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl})\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{16}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{ij}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}\tr_{g}(\partial_{t}g)+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}\tr_{g}(\partial_{t}g)\right).

Now using the self-adjoint property of
\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}
, we simplify the previous expression and obtain the following

\displaystyle\frac{dE_{Ein}}{dt}=\sum_{I=1}^{s}\left(\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{1}{4}\partial_{t}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+k^{tr}_{ij}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}(\partial_{t}k^{tr}_{kl})\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right.\right.

(156)

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{1}{8}u_{ij}[\partial_{t},\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}]u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\frac{1}{2}k^{tr}_{ij}[\partial_{t},\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}]k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right)\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{1}{8}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}(\partial_{t}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\gamma^{ik}\partial_{t}\gamma^{jl})+\frac{1}{2}k^{tr}_{ij}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}_{kl}(\partial_{t}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\gamma^{ik}\partial_{t}\gamma^{jl})\right)\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{1}{8}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}^{I}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\frac{1}{2}k^{tr}_{ij}\mathcal{L}^{I-1}_{g,\gamma}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right)\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}\partial_{t}g\mu_{g}\right).

We will handle the
I=1
case first and then use the inequalities listed in section
4.1
to control the higher order terms. Let us denote the energy corresponding to
I=1
by
E^{1}_{Ein}
. First let us write down the evolution equations for
(u,k^{tr})
as follows

\displaystyle\frac{\partial u_{ij}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{a}Nk^{tr}_{ij}+h^{TT||}_{ij}+\frac{1}{a}X^{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}u_{ij}+\mathcal{F}_{1ij},

(157)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial k^{tr}_{ij}}{\partial t}=-\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(n-1)k^{tr}_{ij}-\frac{1}{a}\nabla[\gamma]_{i}\nabla_{j}\omega+\frac{1}{a}X^{k}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}k^{tr}_{ij}+\frac{1}{2a}N\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{ij}+\mathcal{F}_{2ij},

where
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
are non-linear terms which will be estimated separately

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1ij}=2\frac{\dot{a}}{a}\omega g_{ij}+(L_{Y^{||}+\frac{1}{a}X}\gamma)_{ij}+\frac{1}{a}u_{ik}\nabla[\gamma]_{j}X^{k}+\frac{1}{a}u_{kj}\nabla[\gamma]_{i}X^{k},

(158)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2ij}=-\frac{2}{a}(1+\omega)k^{tr}_{ik}k^{trk}_{j}+\left(a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}\frac{\omega\gamma_{ad}C_{\rho}+N(\gamma_{ad}-2)\delta\rho}{n-1}+\frac{\omega}{a(n-1)}\right)g_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2a}g^{lk}(\nabla[\gamma]_{i}u_{jk}+\nabla[\gamma]_{j}u_{ik}-\nabla[\gamma]_{k}u_{ij})\nabla_{l}\omega-\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(n-2)\omega k^{tr}_{ij}

(159)

\displaystyle-\gamma_{ad}a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+\omega)(C_{\rho}+\delta\rho)v_{i}v_{j}+\frac{1}{a}k^{tr}_{ik}\nabla[\gamma]_{j}X^{k}+\frac{1}{a}k^{tr}_{kj}\nabla[\gamma]_{i}X^{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{a}(1+\omega)\mathfrak{J}_{ij}.

The following lemma yields the estimate for the first order total energy coming from the gravity sector.

Lemma 11:

Let
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
and
B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})\subset H^{s}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}\times H^{s-1}
be a ball for sufficiently small
\delta>0
. Also let
(u,k^{tr},\delta\rho,v)\in B_{\delta}(\mathbf{0})
solve the re-scaled Einstein-Euler-
\Lambda
evolution equations and
E^{1}_{Ein}
be the first order energy of the gravitational sector defined by equation (
85
) for
I=1
. Then
\frac{dE^{1}_{Ein}}{dt}
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\frac{dE^{1}_{Ein}}{dt}=-\frac{(n-1)\dot{a}}{a}\langle k^{tr}|k^{tr}\rangle_{L^{2}}+\mathcal{ER}_{1},

(160)

where
\mathcal{ER}_{1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathcal{ER}_{1}\leq C\left(\frac{1}{a}||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+||Y^{||}+\frac{1}{a}X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||^{3}_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{a}||u||_{H^{s}}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a^{1-n\gamma_{ad}}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})(1+||\delta\rho||_{H^{s-1}})||v||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\left[\frac{\dot{a}}{a}||\omega||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\frac{1}{a}(1+||\omega||_{H^{s+1}})||k^{tr}||_{H^{s-1}}+\frac{1}{a}||X||_{H^{s+1}}||u||_{H^{s}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.(||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}+||k^{tr}||^{2}_{H^{s-1}})\right).

Proof:

\frac{dE^{1}_{Ein}}{dt}
reads

\displaystyle\frac{dE^{1}_{Ein}}{dt}=\int_{\Sigma}(\underbrace{\frac{1}{4}\partial_{t}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+k^{tr}_{ij}(\partial_{t}k^{tr}_{kl})\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}}_{I}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{8}u_{ij}[\partial_{t},\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}]u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl})}_{II}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\Sigma}\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{8}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}(\partial_{t}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\gamma^{ik}\partial_{t}\gamma^{jl})+\frac{1}{2}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}(\partial_{t}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\gamma^{ik}\partial_{t}\gamma^{jl})\right)}_{III}\mu_{g}\right.

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{8}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+\frac{1}{2}k^{tr}_{ij}k^{tr}_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right)\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}\partial_{t}g}_{IV}\mu_{g}.

Once again, we consider collection of terms that would yield necessary cancellations to remove the dangerous terms.

Estimating terms of type
I
:
We first control the terms of type
I
as follows

\displaystyle I=\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{1}{4}\partial_{t}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+k^{tr}_{ij}(\partial_{t}k^{tr}_{kl})\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right)\mu_{g}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(-\frac{2N}{a}k^{tr}_{ij}+\frac{1}{a}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}-h^{TT||}_{ij}+\mathcal{F}_{1ij}\right)\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right.

(161)

\displaystyle\left.+k^{tr}_{ij}\left(-\frac{\dot{a}}{a}(n-1)k^{tr}_{kl}-\frac{1}{a}\nabla[\gamma]_{k}\nabla_{l}\omega+\frac{1}{a}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}k^{tr}_{kl}+\frac{N}{2a}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\mathcal{F}_{2kl}\right)\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right)\mu_{g}.

Now notice that the principal term
k^{tr}_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}
cancels point-wise with its negative counterpart. This is essential since controlling this term requires one order of extra regularity (since in the case of
I=1
, we can not have terms that involve the derivative of
k^{tr}
). We still do have derivatives of
k^{tr}
(and of
u
) in the direction of the shift vector field. However, the derivatives in the direction of the shift vector field
X
are good and can be controlled through integration by parts as follows

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

(162)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\left(-g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}-V^{n}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}u_{kl}-2R[\gamma]_{kplq}u^{pq}\right)\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g},

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\left(-g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}-2R[\gamma]_{kplq}u^{pq}\right)\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g},

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}(X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij})\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}-2\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}R[\gamma]_{kplq}u^{pq}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g},

\displaystyle=\int_{\Sigma}\left(g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}+g^{rs}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\right)\mu_{g}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}X^{m}\left(R[\gamma]^{p}~_{irm}u_{pj}+R[\gamma]^{p}`_{jrm}u_{ip}\right)\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle-2\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}R[\gamma]_{kplq}u^{pq}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}(\nabla[\gamma]_{r}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl})\mu_{g}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl})\mu_{g}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}X^{m}\left(R[\gamma]^{p}~_{irm}u_{pj}+R[\gamma]^{p}`_{jrm}u_{ip}\right)\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle-2\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}R[\gamma]_{kplq}u^{pq}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}(X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}g^{rs}+g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}X^{m}+\frac{1}{2}g^{pn}(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{pn}+\nabla[\gamma]_{p}u_{mn}-\nabla[\gamma]_{n}u_{ap})

\displaystyle\nabla[\gamma]_{r}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}+\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}\nabla[\gamma]_{r}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl})\mu_{g}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma}g^{rs}X^{m}\left(R[\gamma]^{p}~_{irm}u_{pj}+R[\gamma]^{p}`_{jrm}u_{ip}\right)\nabla[\gamma]_{s}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}

\displaystyle-2\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}R[\gamma]_{kplq}u^{pq}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g},

where we have used the SH gauge condition (i.e.,
V=0
) and Stokes’s theorem on the closed manifold
\Sigma
.
Notice that all the terms can be estimated in terms of
E^{1}_{Ein}
since the terms of the type
X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}
have disappeared by the virture of integration by parts and
H^{s}\hookrightarrow W^{1,\infty}
for
s>\frac{n}{2}+1
(we assume
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
throughout). Here we have used the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}
and
V^{i}=g^{kl}(\Gamma[g]^{i}_{kl}-\Gamma[\gamma]^{i}_{kl})=0
in CMCSH gauge. Therefore the term
\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}
is estimated as follows

\displaystyle|\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}|

(163)

\displaystyle\leq C(||X||_{L^{\infty}}||\nabla[\gamma]u||_{L^{\infty}}+||\nabla[\gamma]X||_{L^{\infty}}+||X||_{L^{\infty}})||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}

and following Sobolev embedding (since
s>\frac{n}{2}+2
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\int_{\Sigma}X^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}u_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}|

(164)

\displaystyle\leq C||X||_{H^{s+1}}(1+||u||_{H^{s}})||u||^{2}_{H^{s}}.

The term containing the directional derivative of
k^{tr}
may be reduced to lower-order terms in a similar fashion. Another potentially dangerous term that appears in
I
is the following term

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}h^{TT||}_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}.

(165)

Upon utilizing the self-adjoint property of
\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}
(with respect the inner product (
7
) used in this context), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}h^{TT||}_{ij}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}=\int_{\Sigma}\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}h^{TT||}_{ij}u_{kl}\gamma^{ik}\gamma^{jl}\mu_{g}.

(166)

Now utilizing the definition of
\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}
and the property that
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}h^{TT||}=0
, we will reduce this term to an innocuous term. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}h^{TT||}_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta^{\gamma}_{g}h^{TT||}_{ij}-2R[\gamma]_{ikjl}h^{TT||kl},

(167)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-g^{mn}(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}h^{TT||}_{ij})-V^{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}h^{TT||}_{ij}-2R[\gamma]_{ikjl}h^{TT||kl},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(g^{mn}-\gamma^{mn})(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}h^{TT||}_{ij})-\gamma^{mn}\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}h^{TT||}_{ij}

\displaystyle-2R[\gamma]_{ikjl}h^{TT||kl},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(g^{mn}-\gamma^{mn})(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}h^{TT||}_{ij})+\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}h^{TT||}_{ij},

where we have used the identity
\nabla[\gamma]_{m}(\mu_{g}g^{-1})^{mn}=-V^{n}\mu_{g}
, and set
V^{m}=0
since we are working in CMCSH gauge. Using
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma,\gamma}h^{TT||}=0
(by the definition of
h^{TT||}
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}h^{TT||}_{ij}=-(g^{mn}-\gamma^{mn})(\nabla[\gamma]_{m}\nabla[\gamma]_{n}h^{TT||}_{ij}).

(168)

Notice that we have already gained a factor of
g-\gamma
in the expression of
\mathcal{L}_{g,\gamma}h^{TT||}
.
Now we will exploit the shadow gauge condition to obtain an expression for
h^{TT||}
. The shadow gauge together with the expression (
69
) of an
\mathcal{N}-
tangent vector
\partial_{q^{\alpha}}\gamma
reads

\displaystyle\langle g-\gamma|\frac{\partial\gamma}{\partial q^{\alpha}}\rangle_{L^{2}}
